2026-08-14  Collision FX (standalone renderer code, no RVA, no ladder move)  SUSPECTED OVER-FIRING INVESTIGATED -> NO DEFECT  The "2164 skids/race vs the 251 the calibration was tuned to" alarm raised earlier today was an INSTRUMENTATION ARTIFACT, not a behaviour change. Root cause: fx_skids_/fx_sparks_ were single counters summed across all four car slots while the MASHED_FX_DEBUG line printed only slot 1 inputs -- so a STATIONARY slot-1 car appeared to emit thousands of skids (log samples 36+ showed spd=0.00 skidI=0.00 with the counter still climbing 20-40 per sample) and no figure was attributable to any car. FIXED: fx_skids_[4]/fx_sparks_[4] per slot, debug line emitted for ALL slots with that slot own inputs and own counters. Re-measured distribution is unremarkable: slot0=772 slot1=358 slot2=241 slot3=378, player highest and genuinely cornering (skidI=2.82 corner=18.72 > kCorner=6.65). The old total was further inflated by MASHED_DRIVE_HOLD extending the race past the natural one the 251 came from -- the two numbers were never like-for-like. LOAD-BEARING CHECK: cumulative emit counts cannot answer "is the rate starving the shared pool", so pool occupancy was instrumented via the existing ParticleSystem::alive() and added to the debug line: 204 samples, min=0 median=130 p90=165 MAX=175 against kPool=1200 (14.6%). No starvation, no recycle pressure. Thresholds STAND at the current scaffold drive model; the D2 re-measure remains for when MASHED_REAL_PHYSICS makes vel[] carry real signal. Evidence verify/fx_verify/fx_debug_perslot_occupancy.txt.

The original leaves cnt (copied byte count) in EAX at RET; the single caller FUN_005ab710 consumes it at 0x005ab7f7 ADD EBP,EAX and 0x005ab7f9 ADD EDI,EAX to advance the audio stream cursor. The void port returned garbage in EAX, so with the hook installed the audio stream pump corrupted during track load and the WARPED race wedged at phase 2 - the full-hook-set statediff config failed 0/~19 boots. FOUND BY INDEX BISECTION, not by reading code: MASHED_HOOK_LO/HI over the 1245-entry registry (~20s/boot, 13 boots, MASHED_HOOK_MANIFEST for the index->name table) converged on idx 946 = Ring5ab980, which reproduced the wedge INSTALLED ALONE - the strongest single-culprit confirmation. U-6701 HAD THE DIAGNOSIS ALL ALONG: the plate note already said the caller folds the EAX return into the byte cursor and called it 'non-blocking'. It was not - an implicit-EAX return consumed by the caller is ABI, the same class as U-9025 (AudioThreadDescInit) and the installed-hook-ABI-mismatch lesson: a synthetic diff CANNOT catch a caller-side register contract. FIX: port returns cnt (PromoLoop_sessionB.cpp). VERIFIED LIVE: culprit-alone boot healthy (race runs, 533 statediff frames); full set 0/~19 -> 5/6 boots. 2026-07-31  0x0046d510  VehicleVelocityWorldGet  C2->C3 IN-RACE  out3_idx audit FULLY CLOSED  Orchestrator iter21. The last of the four demoted rows is re-verified, so all four are back at C3 and the audit is finished. THE EARLY-WINDOW ATTEMPT FAILED FOR A LANE REASON, NOT A PORT REASON, and the distinction is the whole point: that lane force-calls BEFORE boot, when neither the matrix at 0x00614708 nor the vehicle records exist, so FUN_004c3df0 dereferenced unset state and 8 of 11 cases died with an access violation ON BOTH SIDES. A both-sides-identical failure is a DEAD RUN - neither evidence of a defect nor of correctness - and calling it RED would have been as wrong as calling it GREEN. Re-run on the in-race diff_template.js lane with cache_setter_observe: GREEN 4/4, four distinct fingerprints. TWO DESIGN DECISIONS CARRY THIS RUN. (1) THE OUT POINTER IS AN ABSOLUTE ADDRESS - slot 15's velocity block 0x0088e2b0, which no test below uses as a destination - because cache_setter_observe can only observe absolute addresses, and the payload here is what the function writes THROUGH ITS OUT POINTER. Observing the transform's own destination at 0x00881f74+idx*0xd04 instead would have been easier and WRONG: it would not catch a port that copied from the wrong offsets, which is precisely the defect class out3_idx let through and this re-verification exists to close. (2) THE MATRIX IS SEEDED TO IDENTITY, along with the source vector per test. Without seeding, determinism would rest on the two back-to-back calls observing the same live vehicle state, and a frame boundary landing between them would produce a spurious RED - a flaky test being its own kind of false evidence. Identity was chosen because it is LAYOUT-AGNOSTIC: the same bit pattern whether the matrix is row-major or column-major, so the run makes no assumption about a storage order that has not been established. It also makes the output equal the seeded input, so every fingerprint is directly readable: idx0 -> 1.0/2.0/3.0, idx1 -> 4.0/5.0/6.0, idx2 -> -1.5/0.25/8.0, and idx16 (out of range) -> the out block's 0x5EED0000/1/2 sentinel SURVIVED INTACT, proving the bounds check bites and nothing was written. A port with a wrong stride, a wrong source offset or a wrong copy offset shows up immediately against those. BOTH SIDES CALL THE SAME ORIGINAL FUN_004c3df0, so the transform itself is not under test - what is under test is the bounds check, the three address computations and the copy, i.e. exactly what out3_idx could not see. Contrived state, C3-grade: every seeded and observed address including the world matrix is snapshotted and restored by the handler, so the live race is unperturbed after the run. C3 not C4 - a synthetic A/B on seeded state is not a canonical scenario. FINAL AUDIT TALLY: 4 rows demoted (2 from C4, 2 from C3), ALL FOUR now restored to C3, out3_idx retired in both harnesses, and net C4 185->183 stands because a synthetic force-call does not re-establish the canonical-scenario half of C4 for the two rows that held it.
2026-07-31  0x0046d740 C2->C3 + arg_type out3_idx RETIRED  out3_idx audit CLOSED  Orchestrator iter21. The last of the four demoted rows is re-verified and the handler that caused the whole episode is gone. 0x0046d740 IS A SETTER, which is why ptr_out_table_get did not fit it: its observable is the GLOBAL BLOCK it writes (dst = 0x008816e4 + idx*0xd04, dst[0..2] = src[0..2]), not an out buffer, and out3_idx observed NEITHER. Re-run under cache_setter_observe: GREEN 4/4 with FOUR DISTINCT fingerprints. THE DESIGN DECISION THAT MAKES IT NON-DEGENERATE is where the SOURCE comes from. A null entry in that handler's args list passes the shared scratch buffer, which the handler does NOT seed - so every in-range test would have copied the same zero bytes, all three fingerprints would have been identical, and a port that wrote a constant zero would have passed. A counter on the .asi export is the cleaner primitive: the export is reachable only THROUGH the installed JMP, so a non-zero count is positive proof the port executed. NEXT, concretely: make the .asi-export counter arm LATE (after the menu is up and dinput8 has loaded the .asi) rather than at attach time, then re-run the canonical race. NEW ARG_TYPE `stub_dispatch_observe` generalises "plant a recorder where the function expects a callee, then compare what it received". vtable_table_dispatch had done this for one hard-wired layout; three more rows needed the same primitive with different plumbing, so the plumbing became CONFIG — a stub planted in a fake vtable (0x004cbb50, 0x00550950) or passed straight as an argument (0x005af200). Nothing real is ever dispatched, which is the point: for a release thunk or a stream-read dispatch, letting the true callee run would touch live engine objects. Pointer arguments the recorder receives are normalised to "b<i>+<off>" against the scratch buffers, since the two sides get different addresses. A per-test `stub_ret` was REQUIRED rather than a fixed constant: 0x004cbb50's entire behaviour is a single dispatch, so a fixed layout leaves nothing to vary across seeds and the run would have been degenerate by construction; varying the recorder's return also tests the thunk's return passthrough, which is its whole second half. Gates: 0x004cbb50 callers 0x0049ab10 + 0x004f8730 C2; 0x00550950 callers 0x004cbd30 RwStreamRead C3 + 0x005ab710 C2; 0x005af200 callers 0x005a8560/0x005af260/0x005af600/0x005af740 all C2. All three callee-halves are vacuous under the indirect-dispatch clause (2026-07-31), whose requirement that the A/B exercise the dispatch itself via a stubbed target recording its arguments is precisely what this handler does. 2026-07-31  0x004a2b60  sprintf  IDENTIFIED as MSVC CRT, removed from the port queue (no C-level change)  Orchestrator iter17. This row had been spec'd SPEC_COMPLETE and authorable by a handler brief; it is `sprintf` from the MSVC CRT and must not be ported. The identification is unambiguous from the disassembly — it builds a fake `FILE` on the stack (`[EBP-0x20]` `_ptr` = dst, `[EBP-0x18]` `_base` = dst, `[EBP-0x1c]` `_cnt` = 0x7fffffff, `[EBP-0x14]` `_flag` = 0x42), takes the varargs pointer with `LEA EAX,[EBP+0x10]`, and hands all three to `_output` (FUN_004a504f, already recorded as the printf format core). On return it NUL-terminates via `AND byte ptr [EAX],0` at 0x004a2ba0, or calls FUN_004a4ea8 on overflow, and returns the character count in EDI. That is the textbook MSVC sprintf shape. Decisive corroboration: 0x004a2b60 falls inside the FidDB-attested library band `msvc-crt-main span=0x004a0000..0x004b4000`, which the hooks.csv row for 0x004a4ea8 explicitly marks "do NOT promote". THE GENERAL DEFECT, which is the reason this is worth a CHANGELOG entry: **a screening brief asks whether a function can be TESTED; it never asks whether the function should be PORTED.** Nothing upstream applied a library-band filter, so six gate-pool rows sat inside the CRT band and only the two already carrying a LIBRARY_SKIP tag had been caught — 0x004a2b60, 0x004a2c2f, 0x004af32d and 0x004a77eb were all being processed as ordinary port candidates. 0x004a77eb is the sharpest case: re/orchestrator/gate_verdicts_manual.tsv had called it "the one row of the twelve genuinely still unscreened", when in fact it is library residue and never needed screening at all. Fix: scripts/orch_rank_gate.py now carries a LIBRARY_BANDS table (msvc-crt-main, d3dx9-psgp, libpng-zlib, qhull-2002.1, msvc-crt-tail) and a band hit OVERRIDES whatever a brief concluded, forcing LIBRARY_SKIP/DEFER. Pool tally moves accordingly: DEFER 2 -> 6, NEEDS_NEW_HANDLER 22 -> 20, NEEDS_GHIDRA 31 -> 30, MUTATOR_LANE 44 -> 43. 2026-07-31  0x00482900  FUN_00482900  C2 (plate + hooks.csv note CORRECTED, no C-level change)  Orchestrator iter17. Not a promotion — a factual correction to an existing C2 whose mechanical description was materially wrong. The old plate and hooks.csv note both described `int FUN_00482900(void)` — zero arguments — with `FUN_004a2c48` as a "checkpoint/frame count getter". Both claims are false. `FUN_004a2c48` is **`__ftol`**, the MSVC CRT x87 round-to-int64 helper, already C3 and byte-identical at Math/FPURound.cpp (hooks.csv:254). It takes its input in **ST0**, so Ghidra — which cannot see x87 register arguments — rendered the call as `FUN_004a2c48()` and, because this function's own two arguments are consumed only by x87 instructions, reported the whole function as void-parameter. The real signature is `int f(float param_1, int param_2)`: `FLD [ESP+4]` / `FMUL [0x005cc728] (=60.0f)` / `FIDIV [ESP+0xc]` / `CALL __ftol` / `LEA ESI,[EAX+EAX*8]` / `LEA ESI,[ESI*4+0x19c]`, i.e. `frames = (int)(param_1 * 60.0f / param_2)` then `size = frames*36 + 412`. Note the `*36` is a strength-reduced LEA pair, not an `imul`, and the divisor is read with FIDIV so param_2 is an INTEGER. The old plate's arithmetic conclusion (`count*36+412`) was right; its derivation was not — the count is COMPUTED from two arguments, not fetched from anywhere. A port written from the old plate would have been `int f(void)` calling a getter that does not exist. Found by the iter16 clause re-screen, which flagged two plates contradicting each other about FUN_004a2c48 ("checkpoint/frame count getter" vs a bare [STUB]); hooks.csv turned out to agree with neither. Both plates corrected; the secondary one (breadth_unmapped_0048x) additionally cited the call at 0x00482904, which is the `PUSH ESI` — the call is at 0x0048290f. SYSTEMIC NOTE, not fixed here: 246 plates under re/analysis/ contain the zero-argument form `FUN_004a2c48()`. That rendering is expected and not itself an error — it is the decompiler hiding an ST0 argument. The defect is only in plates that go on to INVENT a semantic for it; 25 plates correctly identify it as ftol. Detection recipe for a future sweep: `grep -rl "FUN_004a2c48()" re/analysis/` intersected with plates that do NOT mention ftol, then check each for an assigned meaning. The meaning of param_1/param_2 is left [UNCERTAIN] — the 60.0f is consistent with a seconds-to-frames conversion, but nothing in this body names either argument, so it must be resolved from the three call sites.

A dispatch pair that is BYTE-IDENTICAL apart from one displacement byte — `mov ecx,[eax+4]` at 0x005b10a8 vs `mov ecx,[eax+8]` at 0x005b10e8 — so one handler with a CONFIG value covered both: `entry = *(holder + K) + idx*8; entry[0](a1, *(u16*)(entry+4), a4)`. Each 8-byte table entry carries a function pointer at +0 and a 16-bit datum at +4 passed as the dispatched call's MIDDLE argument; nothing names that datum, so it is reported as a raw u16 with no meaning assigned. ARGUMENT ORDER was read off the stack rather than from the decompiler: the two PUSHes before `mov eax,[esp+0xc]` (0x005b10bb) shift ESP, so that read reaches the ORIGINAL [esp+4] — a1 is pushed LAST and is therefore the dispatched call's arg1, a4 pushed FIRST and is arg3. The `xor eax,eax` / `mov ax` pair zero-extends the u16; it is never sign-extended. NEW ARG_TYPE `vtable_table_dispatch`: nothing real is dispatched — the handler writes its own NativeCallback into entry[0], so the call lands on a recorder, and the recorded triple proves the *8 stride, the +4 aux read, the indirection offset and the argument order in a single observation. Varying idx over 0/1/3/7 is what exercises the stride — a wrong stride still passes idx 0 and fails the rest — and aux16 0xffff recording as 0000ffff confirms the zero-extension. Since the two rows differ ONLY in the offset, a copy-paste port would read a different entry and record a different aux16, so the expected failure mode is covered directly. New INDIRECT-DISPATCH clause added to re/CONFIDENCE.md (2026-07-31, user-approved): where a function's only callee is such an indirect call, the callee-half is vacuous as for a leaf, because the call graph contains no unknown *function* — only data — and which function runs is a property of the caller's data at run time, not of this function's semantics. The clause REQUIRES that the A/B exercise the dispatch itself via a stubbed target recording its arguments, which is exactly what was done here; without that requirement the thing that makes the callee unknown would also be the thing left untested. The reimpl was `void __cdecl StructInit418a00(unsigned char* p)`, so the installed inline-JMP took a caller stack-garbage word as `p` and wrote 0x6c bytes through it. The source comment even documented the register ABI and then dismissed it ("the diff drives the original via an ESI trampoline and the reimpl via a stack-arg pointer") — that reasoning is about the HARNESS's call, never about the installed hook's real callers. FIXED by a naked marshalling shim StructInit418a00_RegAbi (`push esi; call StructInit418a00; add esp,4; ret`) which is what RH_ScopedInstall now registers; the C body keeps its stack ABI so re/frida/hooks_registry.py is unaffected. EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=0x00418A00 BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 @ 6.88/7.23/7.23 s; AFTER = 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. ALSO FIXED on disassembly evidence alone: 0x00421060 StructZero421060 is the identical pattern (0x00421062 `PUSH ESI`, 0x0042106d..0x00421076 `MOV [ESI+n],EAX`, nothing on the stack) but does NOT AV in isolation (3/3 BOOTS 16 s both before and after) — a wrong ABI that happens to land on writable garbage is still a wrong ABI. The C body keeps its stack ABI because hooks_registry.py drives the export `Find42ad90` with stack args (arg_type edx_ebx_edi_find sets registers on the ORIGINAL side only). EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=0x0042AD90 BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 @ 8.15/8.40/8.42 s; AFTER = 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. Found by bisect_hook_index.ps1 index 951. 2026-07-26  0x004b40c0 (+ 0x004b4140)  RenderElemArrayCopy / RenderElemArrayCopyAll  BUGFIX (stays C2)  FOURTH confirmed boot-AV crasher, RESOLVED — a LOST RETURN VALUE, a new sub-class of the ABI-mismatch family. The prior session correctly refused to guess: the body was faithful to the cited ASM and the dump signature differed (eip=0x004b52ac, WRITE violation on 0x004a467c inside MASHED's own code/.rdata, not the near-null +0x44 dispatch). Ghidra resolves it exactly. FUN_004b40c0 loads `MOV EAX,dword ptr [EDX+0x24]` at 0x004b40c4 — the element COUNT — before any branch, and NO path afterwards clobbers EAX (the loop counter is ECX at 0x004b40e0, the scratch is EDI), so EAX still holds the count at the RET at 0x004b40e7: the function implicitly RETURNS the count. That value propagates through the tail-JMP adjustor FUN_004b4130 (0x004b413b `JMP 0x004b40c0`) and through the wrapper FUN_004b4140 (whose only post-CALL instruction is `ADD ESP,0x8` at 0x004b414c). The SOLE caller of 0x004b4140 is FUN_004b5260, which consumes it at 0x004b5281 `MOV ESI,EAX` and uses it as the trip count of the write loop at 0x004b52a3..0x004b52af — where 0x004b52ac is `MOV dword ptr [EDX+0x4],EDI`, the exact faulting instruction from the dump. Both reimpls were declared `void`, leaving EAX undefined, so FUN_004b5260 iterated a garbage count, read pointers off the end of its 0x44-byte frame and wrote through them. FIXED: RenderElemArrayCopy is now `int __cdecl` returning `count`, RenderElemArrayCopyAll is now `int __cdecl` forwarding it. EVIDENCE: MASHED_HOOK_ONLY BEFORE = 3/3 AV 0xC0000005 (prior session, ~6.6 s); AFTER = 0x004b40c0 3/3 BOOTS 16 s and 0x004b4140 3/3 BOOTS 16 s. LESSON: a `void` reimpl is only safe when the original's EAX is provably dead at every RET; an original that merely leaves a useful value in EAX has an implicit return that the callers may depend on.

2026-07-26  0x005ab380  AudioRwsChunkHeaderRead  BUGFIX (stays C2)  **ROOT CAUSE of the pc=0x44 boot AV, found and FIXED.** The port was an 8-byte STACK BUFFER OVERFLOW: the original reads 0xc bytes into the lowest of three CONTIGUOUS stack slots (-0xc/-0x8/-0x4), so `read(&local_c, 0xc)` legitimately fills all three; the transcription copied that idiom literally into three SEPARATE C++ locals and passed `&local_c`. C++ guarantees nothing about the placement/order/padding of distinct locals, so the callee wrote 12 bytes into a 4-byte object, smashing 8 bytes of the caller's frame, while local_8/local_4 were read effectively uninitialised. Fixed by reading into an explicit `std::uint32_t hdr[3]` (contiguous by construction) and copying out. LESSON: a stack layout the original relies on must be modelled EXPLICITLY, never inferred from adjacent local declarations. Repo-wide sweep found this exact pattern in only ONE place. EVIDENCE (single-hook before/after, MASHED_HOOK_ONLY=AudioRwsChunkHeaderRead, WaitForExit-measured): BEFORE = AV 0xC0000005 @ 4.71 s / 4.61 s; AFTER = BOOTS 18.02 s / 18.00 s. HOW IT WAS FOUND: static analysis (Ghidra) pinned the faulting instruction to 0x005ab237 `CALL dword ptr [EAX+0x44]` in the RW-audio stream-handler command dispatcher, validated by a dual-register match against the dump (eax=0x02e0c884=obj, ecx=0x0000080a=[[obj]+0xc]); the object was a VALID heap pointer with only its +0x44 handler slot garbage — consistent with a neighbouring frame being smashed. Independently, a hook-INDEX binary search using the built-in MASHED_HOOK_LO/HI knobs converged on index 86 = 0x005AB380 = this function. Static and dynamic evidence agreed. ALSO CORRECTED: the 2026-07-25 conclusion that subset installs 'do not dodge it' is FALSE — full .asi with MASHED_HOOK_HI=0 (zero hooks installed) BOOTS 18 s, so the trigger is hook INSTALLATION, not .asi static-init. NOT DONE: at least one MORE independent crasher remains (full hook set still AVs @ ~4.5-4.9 s after this fix); re-run the index bisect to find it. 2026-07-13  M1 tail item 9 -- D-11058 resolved: Lap_Line_End has no consumer (no confidence change; DEFERRED.md D-11058)  M1 TAIL CLOSED (final item). Ghidra pool14 read-only. String "Lap_Line_End" (0x005cdda4) sole xref 0x00440dc0, a Lua-command registration loop binding it to handler LAB_0047a7d0, which writes sentinel -1 into `[DAT_006bf1d8 + counter(DAT_006bf1dc)*4 + 0x159c0]` -- the same flat array the sibling "Lap_Line" handler FUN_0047a790 appends real gate indices into (terminator dialect matches re/analysis/formats/track_anim_data.md:200,205). Exhaustive proof of no consumer: program-wide `search_instructions("159c0")` = exactly 3 hits total (Lap_Line/Lap_Line_End/Lap_Line_Change writes only, zero reads); `reference_to` on both backing globals (counter 0x006bf1dc: 7 refs, base ptr 0x006bf1d8: 9 refs) shows every access confined to the write-time handler cluster 0x0047a790-0x0047a860, nothing downstream reads the array or its length. Array is write-only across the entire binary. No code change: TrackData.cpp::LapData::Parse already discards Lap_Line_End as a no-op terminator, matching original's own write-only semantics bit-for-bit in effect. hooks.csv/STUBS.md/UNCERTAINTIES.md untouched (no function promotion occurred).

2026-07-11  M1 tail item 8 -- gate D3 (split-screen local MP) scope decision: OUT for v1.0 (no confidence change; DEFERRED.md D-11063)  User decision, made after reviewing an evidence brief (worker-account survey): the informal "WS-I" umbrella splits into two clusters -- (a) the split-screen render pipeline (PerPlayerViewportRender 0x00420050 + per-player shadow/tire-mark/geometry/viewport helpers, CameraTransitionStateMachine 0x0042c960) reversed to C1-C2 only, nothing ported; (b) MP mode/rule-selection logic (IsMultiplayerMode 0x00430760, SplitScreenTrackAssignment 0x00430830, HudSlotTypePlayer2 0x00430ab0, LocalPlayerSlotCheck 0x00436810, MenuMenusBD 0x0040b460) already C3/C4 ported+verified, unaffected by this deferral. One original-side unknown remains open (U-1908, per-player viewport-rect assignment unlocated). The standalone's renderer/camera/input/HUD are single-viewport-by-construction (TrackRenderer.cpp:3730-3731, TrackRenderer.h:53/103, RaceSession.cpp:93) -- split-screen would be new render+input+HUD work, not a toggle. Cost of deferring per RE_MASTER_PLAN_2026-07.md:196: "one fewer mode in the P-DoD playthrough," no doc claims M1 is blocked without it. MP rule/scoring layer (D-11052/53/54, already RESOLVED) continues to function standalone, verified via the rules oracle against a real 2-logical-entrant match -- this deferral is scoped only to split-screen rendering + a second local human input device. Filed DEFERRED.md D-11063, re-pickup: v1.0 explicitly wants local split-screen as a shipped mode, or U-1908 resolves incidentally and the marginal viewport cost drops. hooks.csv untouched (render-pipeline rows stay C1-C2, MP-rule rows stay C3/C4 exactly as they are) -- this is a port-scope decision, not an RE-confidence change.
